   - Node N
   - Node P

2. **Examine the immediate neighboring nodes of N and P:**
   - Neighboring nodes of N: Y, B, and C
   - Neighboring nodes of P: O, J, and I

3. **Find all paths between N and P:**
   - **Path 1:** N -> B -> H -> K -> F -> E -> L -> M -> J -> P
   - **Path 2:** N -> B -> H -> G -> F -> E -> L -> M -> J -> P
   - **Path 3:** N -> Y -> O -> P
   - **Path 4:** N -> B -> H -> G -> K -> F -> X -> E -> L -> M -> J -> P
   - **Path 5:** N -> B -> H -> G -> F -> E -> L -> M -> J -> P

4. **Identify the bottlenecks in the paths:**
   - To disconnect N and P, we need to remove nodes that are common in these paths.
   - Notice that nodes B and J appear frequently.

5. **Evaluate the minimum cut set:**
   - Removing node B will disconnect paths through B.
   - Removing node J will disconnect paths through J.

6. **Check if removing B and J disconnects N and P:**
   - Removing B disconnects paths: N -> B -> H -> K -> F -> E -> L -> M -> J -> P, N -> B -> H -> G -> K -> F -> E -> L -> M -> J -> P, and N -> B -> H -> G -> K -> F -> X -> E -> L -> M -> J -> P
   - Removing J disconnects paths: N -> B -> H -> K -> F -> E -> L -> M -> J -> P, N -> B -> H -> G -> K -> F -> E -> L -> M -> J -> P, and N -> Y -> O -> P

By removing nodes B and J, all paths between N and P are disconnected. Therefore, the minimum number of nodes to remove is 2.
